Let's design internal objective:
def fast_sequence_score(a): a np array float nonneg. n=len. S=a.sum(); if S<0.01 inf. Compute conv. For n maybe up to 20000, use FFT: N=1; while N<2*n-1: N*=2. f=np.fft.rfft(a,N); conv=np.fft.irfft(f*f,N)[:2*n-1]. Due to floating errors, conv can have tiny negatives; max conv. But for sequences with large dynamic range, FFT precision maybe issue. We can use direct np.convolve for n<5000 exact faster? np.convolve direct in C is okay. For search many evals, FFT good for n≥2000. We can also compute max convolution via direct for small. Use np.maximum.reduce? We'll implement score optional.

Implement fast_score(a):
- n = len(a)
- S = np.sum(a)
- if S < 0.01 return inf
- if n <= 3000 maybe use np.convolve(a,a) for exact? But many evals; use FFT for n>2000. For n=4498, FFT. Need choose FFT length M=1 << (2*n-2).bit_length().
- fa = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
- conv = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, M)[:2*n-1]
- max_b = float(np.max(conv))
- return 2*n*max_b/(S*S)

Let's code shapes:
def make_shapes(n):
    t = np.linspace(0,1,n)
    shapes = {}
    shapes[0] = np.ones(n)
    for q in [0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0]:
       raw = np.maximum(0,1-t)**q
       raw[raw<=0]=0? If q noninteger and t=1, 0^q=0 okay. mean=np.mean(raw); if mean>0: shapes[q]=raw/mean
    raw_sing = 1/np.sqrt(np.arange(1,n+1)); shapes['sing']=raw_sing/raw_sing.mean()
    raw_exp = np.exp(-np.linspace(0,4,n)); shapes['exp']=raw_exp/raw_exp.mean()
Return list of (name, array). All mean approx1.

Base b:
def central_binomial(n):
    b=np.zeros(n); b[0]=1; for k in range(1,n): b[k]=b[k-1]*(2*k-1)/(2*k)
Return.

Candidate from params:
def build_candidate(n, base, shape_list, coeffs):
    a=base.copy()
    inv=1/np.sqrt(n)
    for c,s in zip(coeffs, shape_list): if c!=0: a += c*inv*s
    return a

Let's implement fft_score:
def objective_from_array(a):
    n=a.size
    s=a.sum()
    if s < 1e-8: return inf
    if n < 64:
       conv=np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
       N=1
       m=2*n-1
       while N < m: N <<=1
       fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)
       # only first m, but max over all N includes zeros after m? irfft length N; entries after m should be ~0, but numerical maybe small positive? To be safe, conv=conv[:m]
    max_b=float(np.max(conv))
    if max_b <0: max_b=0
    return 2.0*n*max_b/(s*s)
For n=4096, N=8192. fa*fa complex. irfft returns real N. Good.

def search_for_n(n, seed, time_limit):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    base=make_base(n)
    shapes=make_shapes(n)
    inv=np.sqrt(n)
    scaled_shapes=[s/inv for s in shapes]
    best_a=base.copy()
    best_score=fast_objective(best_a)
    best_single=[]
    for idx,sh in enumerate(shapes):
       # scan c
       cs=np.linspace(0,6,61)
       local_best=0; local_score=best_score
       for c in cs:
          if c==0: continue
          a=base + c*scaled
          sc=fast_objective(a)
          if sc < local_score: ...
       # refine if local_best>0
       lo=max(0,local_best-1.0); hi=local_best+1.0
       cs2=np.linspace(lo,hi,31)
       ...
       best_single.append((local_score, idx, local_best))
       if local_score < best_score: update best_a/base+c*scaled
    sort best_single; top3 = [idx for score<best_single[0]? maybe top3 finite]
    coeffs=np.zeros(len(labels?)) choose top three dimension.

def refine_perturb(a, budget):
   n=a.size; best=a; best_sc=fast_objective(a); rng...
   while time<deadline:
      cand=best.copy()
      width int rng.integers(32, n//8)
      start rng.integers(0,n-width)
      factor=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.05))
      cand[start:start+width]*=factor
      # maybe sym? no.
      cand=np.maximum(cand,0)
      sc=fast_objective(cand)
      if sc<best_sc: best=cand...
   return best

Let's design a deterministic "max coefficient flattening" algorithm for small n:
Given a (sum1). We want minimize M=max b. This is equivalent to minimizing L∞ of b. We can use subgradient descent on a with sum constraint:
- Let active mask where b > M - tol.
- Gradient of M approx average gradient over active: g_i = (1/|A|) ∂b_k/∂a_i.
- Move a = a - η g, but also may need increase? Since sum fixed and we are minimizing M, moving opposite gradient should reduce M. But a must nonnegative. Use multiplicative: log a -= η (g_i - mean(g)) to keep sum approx.
- This is like equalizing peaks.

def flatten_sequence(a, iterations, eta):
    a=a.copy(); a[a<0]=0; a/=a.sum()
    n=len(a)
    for it in range(iterations):
       b=np.convolve(a,a)
       M=b.max()
       tol=max(M*1e-6,1e-12)
       act=b > M - M*0.05? maybe top 20%
       g=np.zeros(n)
       for k in active:
          # derivative of b_k wrt a
          # b_k = ∑_{i=max(0,k-n+1)}^{min(n-1,k)} a_i a_{k-i}
          valid i range
          # g_i += a_{k-i} if i != k-i else a_{k-i} (but derivative of sum over i: as above)
          for i in range(lo,hi+1):
             g[i] += a[k-i]
             if i != k-i:
                 g[i] += a[k-i]  # same value? Wait a[k-i] same; adding twice.
```

def small_sequence_search(n, seed, time_limit):
    rn=...
    best=None; best_sc=inf
    starts = [central_binomial(n), np.ones(n), rn.lognormal..., maybe triangular]
    for start in starts:
       a=normalize(start)
       # local flatten with 2000 iterations
       for it in range(2000):
          b=np.convolve(a,a); M=b.max(); sc=2*n*M
          if sc<best_sc: best=a.copy()
          active = b > M*0.9? choose top min(n, count)
          g=compute_derivative(a, active_indices)
          if g.max()==0: break
          g=(g-g.mean())/ (g.std()+1e-12)
          eta = eta0/(1+it/100)
          new = a*np.exp(-eta*g) # since g mean zero
          new = np.maximum(new,0); new/=new.sum()
          # maybe accept if sc lower when compared to current
          sc2=normalized_score(new)
          if sc2 < current_sc: a=new; current=sc2
          else: maybe if best? no
       # after flatten, do random perturbation and repeat
    return best
```

Derivative:
```
def derivative_convolution(a, ks):
   n=len(a); g=np.zeros(n)
   for k in ks:
      lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
      for i in range(lo,hi+1):
         g[i] += 2*a[k-i]
   return g
```
This is O(active*n). Good.

Let's detail small search:
```
def normalized_objective_array(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-12: return inf
    a=a/s
    b=np.convolve(a,a)
    return 2*len(a)*float(np.max(b))
```
Small n use direct conv.

Flatten function:
```
def flatten_sequence(start, n, seed, iters=2000, eta0=0.25):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed+n)
    a=np.maximum(start,0); a=a.sum()...
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=normalized_objective_array(a)
    cur=a.copy(); cur_sc=best_sc
    active_count=max(3, min(2*n, 2*int(np.sqrt(n))+1))
    for it in range(iters):
       b=np.convolve(cur,cur); M=max; sc=2nM
       if sc<best_sc: best=cur.copy(); best_sc=sc
       if M<=0: break
       # choose top active
       if active_count >= len(b): idx=np.arange(len(b))
       else: idx=np.argpartition(b, -active_count)[-active_count:]
       g=zeros
       for k in idx:
          lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
          # vectorized?
          for i in range(lo,hi+1): g[i]+=2*cur[k-i]
       gm=g.mean(); gs=g.std()
       if gs<1e-12: break
       eta=eta0/(1+it/200)
       # To avoid extinction, add small floor
       delta = (g-gm)/(gs+1e-12)
       new = cur * np.exp(-eta*delta)
       # occasionally random perturbation
       if it % 50 == 0:
           new *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.05,n))
       new = np.maximum(new,1e-12) # floor to keep positive
       new /= new.sum()
       new_sc=normalized_objective_array(new)
       # Accept if better than current, or occasionally if better than best? Greedy
       if new_sc < cur_sc:
           cur=new; cur_sc=new_sc
       elif new_sc < best_sc and rng.random()<0.1:
           new=... # accept downhill? actually best_sc.
    # final polish: coordinate? maybe not
    return best, best_sc
```

```
def flatten_sequence(start, n, max_iters, eta0, deadline, seed):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = np.asarray(start, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 0: a = np.ones(n)/n
    else: a /= s
    cur = a.copy()
    best = a.copy()
    cur_sc = small_objective(cur)
    best_sc = cur_sc
    active_count = max(3, min(2*n-1, int(5*np.sqrt(n))))
    stale=0
    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
        b = np.convolve(cur, cur)
        M = float(np.max(b))
        sc = 2.0*n*M
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc; best = cur.copy()
        if M <=0: break
        if b.size <= active_count:
            idx = np.arange(b.size)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(b, -active_count)[-active_count:]
        g = np.zeros(n)
        for k in idx:
            lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
            hi = min(n - 1, k)
            ii = np.arange(lo, hi + 1)
            g[ii] += 2.0 * cur[k - ii]
        gm = g.mean()
        gs = g.std()
        if gs < 1e-14:
            break
        eta = eta0 / (1.0 + it / 100.0)
        delta = (g - gm) / gs
        # Limit delta to avoid extreme
        delta = np.clip(delta, -3.0, 3.0)
        new = cur * np.exp(-eta * delta)
        if it % 50 == 0:
            new *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, n))
        new = np.maximum(new, 1e-15)
        ns = new.sum()
        if ns <=0: continue
        new /= ns
        new_sc = small_objective(new)
        if new_sc < cur_sc:
            cur = new; cur_sc = new_sc; stale=0
        else:
            stale += 1
            if stale > 300:
                stale=0
                cur = best.copy()
                cur *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.15,n))
                cur = np.maximum(cur,1e-15); cur/=cur.sum()
                cur_sc = small_objective(cur)
                if cur_sc > best_sc*1.05:
                    cur=best.copy(); cur_sc=best_sc
        if it % 500 == 0:
            # local polish? maybe print
            pass
    # final coordinate small? skip
    return best, best_sc
```
Note: The update `a *= exp(-eta*(g-mean))` decreases high gradient variables. But if high gradient variables are small, relative changes controlled. Good.

`small_objective` normalizes every call (recompute sum). In flatten we already normalized new, but g update floor 1e-15 sum maybe normalized; okay.

def search_length(n, deadline, seed):
    rng=...
    best=None; best_sc=inf
    starts = make_starts(n, rng)
    max_iters = 3000 if n<=32 else 1500 if n<=128 else 800
    for idx,st in enumerate(starts):
       if time.monotonic()>deadline-30: break
       seq, sc = flatten_sequence(st, n, max_iters, 0.25, deadline, seed*1000+n+idx)
       if sc < best_sc: best=sc; best=seq
       print(f"n={n} start={idx} score={sc:.6f} best={best_sc:.6f}")
    return best, best_sc
```

    # two block

```
def fft_objective(a):
    n=a.size
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-12: return np.inf
    # Clip? no
    N=1; m=2*n-1
    while N<m: N<<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)
    if conv.size>m: conv=conv[:m]
    mb=float(np.max(conv))
    if mb<0: mb=0
    return 2.0*n*mb/(s*s)
```

```python
"""
Heuristic search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a))^2.

The program combines several ideas:
- A strong analytic construction: truncated central-binomial tail,
  which is a known strong one-sided non-negative construction.
- Multi-length direct NP-free local search.  For small and medium lengths
  it minimizes the coefficient peak of A(z)^2 while keeping sum(a)=1.
- A multiplicative subgradient / peak-shaping update on polynomial
  coefficients, with random restarts.
- Fallback to the analytic central-binomial sequence if no heuristic
  sequence improves it.

The returned sequence is always non-negative and is validated with the
provided evaluate_sequence() before being returned whenever possible.
"""

import time
import numpy as np


def central_binomial(n: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Coefficients of (1 - z)^(-1/2), truncated to length n."""
    n = int(max(1, n))
    out = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    out[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(1, n):
        out[k] = out[k - 1] * (2 * k - 1) / (2 * k)
    return out


def small_objective(a: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """
    Objective with sum(a) normalized to 1.

    For sum=1 the expression is simply 2*n*max(convolve(a,a)).
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-12:
        return float("inf")
    a = a / s
    conv = np.convolve(a, a)
    mb = float(np.max(conv))
    if mb < 0.0:
        mb = 0.0
    return 2.0 * len(a) * mb


def fft_objective(a: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """
    Fast FFT-based objective for longer sequences.

    This is only used for internal search/selection; the final answer is
    confirmed by evaluate_sequence() when available.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-12:
        return float("inf")

    m = 2 * n - 1
    N = 1
    while N < m:
        N <<= 1

    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    if conv.size > m:
        conv = conv[:m]

    mb = float(np.max(conv))
    if mb < 0.0:
        mb = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * mb / (s * s))


def exact_score(a: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """Use the authoritative evaluator if available."""
    try:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if a.size == 0 or float(a.sum()) < 0.01:
            return float("inf")
        val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a])  # noqa: F821
        return float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def make_starts(n: int, rng: np.random.Generator) -> list:
    """Generate a handful of diverse non-negative starting vectors."""
    n = int(max(2, n))
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    starts = []

    # Strong analytic one-sided seed.
    starts.append(central_binomial(n))

    # Uniform.
    starts.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    # Half-life / inverse-square-root decays from the left.
    starts.append(1.0 / np.sqrt(idx + 1.0))
    starts.append(np.power(0.75, idx))
    starts.append(np.power(0.90, idx))

    # A mild two-block profile.
    arr = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    m = max(1, n // 3)
    arr[:m] = 1.0
    arr[m:2 * m] = 0.45
    starts.append(arr)

    # A mildly increasing profile.
    arr = 1.0 + idx / max(1, n - 1)
    starts.append(arr)

    # Random log-normal profile.
    starts.append(rng.lognormal(0.0, 0.55, size=n).astype(np.float64))

    # Random low-frequency profile.
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    ph0 = rng.uniform(0.0, 6.28)
    ph1 = rng.uniform(0.0, 6.28)
    arr = 0.6 + 0.25 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * t + ph0) + 0.15 * np.cos(4.0 * np.pi * t + ph1)
    starts.append(np.maximum(arr, 1e-3))

    return starts


def flatten_sequence(start, n: int, max_iters: int, eta0: float, deadline: float, seed: int):
    """
    Local peak-shaping search for fixed length n.

    The vector is kept normalized to sum 1.  The update is a multiplicative
    subgradient step on the maximum convolution coefficients of A(z)^2.
    """
    n = int(max(2, n))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    a = np.asarray(start, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size != n:
        info = np.interp(np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n), np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, max(2, a.size)), a)
        a = info
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = a / a.sum()

    cur = a.copy()
    best = a.copy()
    cur_sc = small_objective(cur)
    best_sc = cur_sc

    b_len = 2 * n - 1
    active_count = max(3, min(b_len, int(2.0 * np.sqrt(n)) + 1))
    stale = 0

    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

        b = np.convolve(cur, cur)
        M = float(np.max(b))
        sc = 2.0 * n * M
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best = cur.copy()

        if M <= 0.0:
            break

        if active_count >= b.size:
            active = np.arange(b.size)
        else:
            active = np.argpartition(b, -active_count)[-active_count:]

        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for k in active:
            lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
            hi = min(n - 1, k)
            ii = np.arange(lo, hi + 1)
            g[ii] += 2.0 * cur[k - ii]

        gm = float(g.mean())
        gs = float(g.std())
        if gs < 1e-14:
            break

        eta = eta0 / (1.0 + it / 120.0)
        delta = (g - gm) / gs
        delta = np.clip(delta, -3.0, 3.0)

        new = cur * np.exp(-eta * delta)

        # Occasional stochastic escape.
        if it % 40 == 0:
            new *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.07, size=n))

        new = np.maximum(new, 1e-15)
        ns = float(new.sum())
        if ns <= 1e-15:
            continue
        new /= ns

        new_sc = small_objective(new)

        if new_sc < cur_sc:
            cur = new
            cur_sc = new_sc
            stale = 0
        else:
            stale += 1
            if stale > 250:
                stale = 0
                cur = best.copy()
                cur *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.18, size=n))
                cur = np.maximum(cur, 1e-15)
                cs = float(cur.sum())
                if cs > 1e-15:
                    cur /= cs
                cur_sc = small_objective(cur)
                if cur_sc > best_sc * 1.08:
                    cur = best.copy()
                    cur_sc = best_sc

    return best, best_sc


def search_length(n: int, deadline: float, seed: int):
    """Run a bounded local search for one fixed length."""
    n = int(max(2, n))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 1000003 * n)
    starts = make_starts(n, rng)

    if n <= 12:
        max_iters = 6000
    elif n <= 24:
        max_iters = 4000
    elif n <= 48:
        max_iters = 2500
    elif n <= 128:
        max_iters = 1400
    else:
        max_iters = 800

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    for si, st in enumerate(starts):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        seq, sc = flatten_sequence(st, n, max_iters, 0.25, deadline, seed + 17 * (si + 1))
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq.copy()
            print(f"[len={n}] start={si} score={best_score:.8f}", flush=True)

    return best_seq, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for a low-objective non-negative sequence.

    The function always returns a Python list of non-negative floats.
    It uses at most the supplied wall-clock budget (default 1800 seconds)
    and returns the best sequence found before the end.
    """
    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 1800.0
    budget_s = max(30.0, budget_s)

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(5.0, budget_s - 5.0)

    # A safe analytic fallback.  The length is chosen to keep the external
    # evaluator fast while retaining the asymptotic one-sided shape.
    n_large = 4096
    large_base = central_binomial(n_large)
    large_score = fft_objective(large_base)

    best_seq = large_base.copy()
    best_score = large_score
    print(f"[init] large analytic len={n_large} score={best_score:.8f}", flush=True)

    # Reserve final time for exact validation.
    small_deadline = t0 + max(
        60.0,
        min(
            budget_s - 180.0,
            1200.0
        )
    )

    lengths = [8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 32, 40, 48, 64, 80, 96, 128, 160, 192, 256]
    for n in lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > small_deadline:
            break

        if n <= 16:
            alloc = 70.0
        elif n <= 32:
            alloc = 90.0
        elif n <= 96:
            alloc = 120.0
        else:
            alloc = 150.0

        call_deadline = min(small_deadline, time.monotonic() + alloc)
        if call_deadline <= time.monotonic() + 5.0:
            continue

        seq, sc = search_length(n, call_deadline, int(seed))
        if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc):
            if sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_seq = seq.copy()
                print(f"[search] len={n} new best score={best_score:.8f}", flush=True)
            else:
                print(f"[search] len={n} best so far score={sc:.8f} (not new global)", flush=True)

    # Final exact validation / comparison.  If the local search produced a
    # small sequence, exact evaluation is cheap.  The large analytic base is
    # also validated and chosen if it is better.
    exact_best = exact_score(best_seq)
    exact_large = exact_score(large_base)

    if np.isfinite(exact_large) and (not np.isfinite(exact_best) or exact_large < exact_best):
        best_seq = large_base
        exact_best = exact_large

    # Optional: use the previously supplied global construction if available
    # and still competitive.
    try:
        prev = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if prev is not None:
            prev_arr = np.asarray(prev, dtype=np.float64)
            if prev_arr.ndim == 1 and 0 < prev_arr.size <= 5000:
                prev_arr = np.clip(prev_arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
                if float(prev_arr.sum()) >= 0.01:
                    prev_score = exact_score(prev_arr)
                    if np.isfinite(prev_score) and prev_score < exact_best:
                        best_seq = prev_arr
                        exact_best = prev_score
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Final sanity cleanup.
    best_seq = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    best_seq = np.clip(best_seq, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if best_seq.size == 0 or float(best_seq.sum()) < 0.01:
        best_seq = central_binomial(256)

    # Scale down extremely large entries only if needed by the evaluator.
    if best_seq.size and float(np.max(best_seq)) > 1000.0 / max(1.0, float(best_seq.sum())):
        best_seq = np.clip(best_seq, 0.0, 1000.0)

    print(f"[final](len={best_seq.size}) estimate={best_score:.8f} exact={exact_best:.8f}", flush=True)
    return [float(x) for x in best_seq]
```
